Implement the following plan:

# Plan: Complete Structural Cleanup

## Context

The prior decomposition work broke up 5 large files into subpackages (`source/utils/`, `source/video/`, `source/db/`, `source/wgp/`, `source/task_queue/`, `task_handlers/join/`, `task_handlers/travel/`), but stopped halfway. Five 670-1905 LOC files remain as loose modules in `source/`, the root-level "facade" files still contain full class implementations instead of being thin re-exports, and task_handlers has inconsistent patterns (some facades, some monoliths). This plan finishes the job.

**Why desloppify didn't catch the inconsistency:** It detects individual file size but has no "decomposition completeness" or "structural consistency" detector. It can't tell that `logging_utils.py` at 904 LOC is inconsistent with `common_utils.py` having been decomposed at a similar size. The desloppify issue we filed (#3) includes a proposal for a "monster function" detector, but a "structural consistency" detector (files at similar complexity getting different treatment) would also be valuable.

---

## Phase 1: Complete Remaining Decompositions (5 files)

### 1.1 `source/structure_video_guidance.py` (1905 LOC) → `source/structure/`

3 importers: `task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py`, `travel_segment_processor.py`, `video/travel_guide.py`

| New Module | Contents | ~LOC |
|------------|----------|------|
| `source/structure/loading.py` | `load_structure_video_frames()`, `load_structure_video_frames_with_range()`, resampling | 170 |
| `source/structure/preprocessors.py` | `get_structure_preprocessor()`, `process_structure_frames()`, model init | 180 |
| `source/structure/generation.py` | `create_structure_motion_video()`, `create_structure_guidance_video()`, `create_trimmed_structure_video()`, `create_composite_guidance_video()`, `validate_structure_video_configs()` | 800 |
| `source/structure/segments.py` | `apply_structure_motion_with_tracking()`, `extract_segment_structure_guidance()`, segment positioning helpers | 400 |
| `source/structure/download.py` | `download_and_extract_motion_frames()` | 140 |

`source/structure_video_guidance.py` → re-export facade.

`source/logging_utils.py` → re-export facade. Note: Python has a stdlib `logging` module. Using `source.logging` is fine since it's always qualified, but if there are import conflicts we may need `source.log` instead.

## Phase 2: Flatten Root-Level Facades

### 2.1 `headless_wgp.py` (1649 LOC) → thin facade

Currently contains the full `WanOrchestrator` class with `__init__` (320 LOC) and `generate()` (1080 LOC). The class delegates some operations to `source/wgp/*` but still owns the core logic.

**Action:** Move `WanOrchestrator` class into `source/wgp/orchestrator.py`. The class becomes the centerpiece of the `source/wgp/` package.

`headless_wgp.py` becomes:
```python
"""Backward-compatible import location for WanOrchestrator."""
from source.wgp.orchestrator import WanOrchestrator  # noqa: F401
```

Update `source/wgp/__init__.py` to re-export `WanOrchestrator`.

### 2.2 `headless_model_management.py` (728 LOC) → thin facade

Contains `HeadlessTaskQueue` class and `GenerationTask` dataclass. Delegates queue operations to `source/task_queue/*`.

**Action:** Move `HeadlessTaskQueue` into `source/task_queue/task_queue.py` and `GenerationTask` into `source/task_queue/generation_task.py` (or keep together).

`headless_model_management.py` becomes:
```python
"""Backward-compatible import location for HeadlessTaskQueue."""
from source.task_queue.task_queue import HeadlessTaskQueue, GenerationTask  # noqa: F401
```

---

## Phase 3: Task Handlers Cleanup

### 3.1 Decompose `join_clips_orchestrator.py` (1385 LOC)

This file has a cross-import problem: `edit_video_orchestrator.py` imports 5 shared functions from it. The shared logic should be extracted.

| New Module | Contents | ~LOC |
|------------|----------|------|
| `source/task_handlers/join/shared.py` | Shared core logic used by both orchestrators: `_extract_join_settings_from_payload()`, `_check_existing_join_tasks()`, `_create_join_chain_tasks()`, `calculate_min_clip_frames()`, `validate_clip_frames_for_join()` | 450 |
| `source/task_handlers/join/vlm_enhancement.py` | `_extract_boundary_frames_for_vlm()`, `_generate_join_transition_prompt()`, `_generate_vlm_prompts_for_joins()` | 350 |
| `source/task_handlers/join/orchestrator.py` | `_handle_join_clips_orchestrator_task()`, `_create_parallel_join_tasks()`, `_check_orchestrator_cancelled()`, `_get_video_resolution()` | 500 |

`source/task_handlers/join_clips_orchestrator.py` → re-export facade.

Update `edit_video_orchestrator.py` to import from `source.task_handlers.join.shared` instead.

### 3.2 Move `specialized_handlers.py` into task_handlers

This file has 2 independent task handlers (`handle_extract_frame_task`, `handle_rife_interpolate_task`) that belong with the other task handlers.

- Move to `source/task_handlers/extract_frame.py` and `source/task_handlers/rife_interpolate.py`
- Leave `source/specialized_handlers.py` as re-export facade
- Update `task_registry.py` imports

---

## Phase 4: Merge `debug.py` + `debug/`

`debug.py` is a 189 LOC CLI entry point that imports from `debug.client` and `debug.commands`. Having both `debug.py` and `debug/` at root is a Python antipattern.

**Action:**
- Move `debug.py` → `debug/cli.py` (the CLI dispatcher logic)
- Create `debug/__main__.py` that imports and runs the CLI (enables `python -m debug`)
- Update any scripts that reference `debug.py` to use `python -m debug` instead
- Delete root `debug.py`

---

```bash
import source.logging_utils
```

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user asked to implement a comprehensive structural cleanup plan for the Headless-Wan2GP project. The plan has 5 phases:
   - **Phase 1**: Decompose 5 remaining large files into subpackages (logging_utils.py → source/logging/, structure_video_guidance.py → source/structure/, visualization_utils.py → source/visualization/, vlm_utils.py → source/vlm/, travel_segment_processor.py → move to task_handlers/travel/)
   - **Phase 2**: Flatten root-level facades (headless_wgp.py → thin re-export, headless_model_management.py → thin re-export)
   - **Phase 3**: Task handlers cleanup (decompose join_clips_orchestrator.py into join/shared.py, join/vlm_enhancement.py, join/orchestrator.py; move specialized_handlers.py into task_handlers/)
   - **Phase 4**: Merge debug.py into debug/ package (move to debug/cli.py, create debug/__main__.py)
   - **Phase 5**: Verification (import smoke tests, pytest, circular import checks)
   
   The execution order specified is: logging_utils first (18 importers), then structure_video_guidance, visualization_utils, vlm_utils, travel_segment_processor, join_clips_orchestrator, specialized_handlers, headless_wgp, headless_model_management, debug.py, then verification.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - File decomposition pattern: split monolithic file into subpackage with `__init__.py`, leave original file as thin re-export facade
   - Re-export facade pattern: original file becomes `from source.new_package.module import *` to maintain backward compatibility
   - Python stdlib `logging` module conflict concern: `source/logging/` may shadow stdlib `logging`, may need `source/log/` instead
   - Cross-import problem in join_clips_orchestrator: edit_video_orchestrator imports 5 shared functions that need extraction
   - `source/__init__.py` contains explicit dynamic import references that need updating
   - Vendored directories (Wan2GP/, .tmp_kijai_wanvideowrapper/, upgrade_patches/) must NOT be modified
   - The project uses `source/constants.py` for shared byte constants (BYTES_PER_MB, BYTES_PER_GB)
   - ComponentLogger and SafeComponentLogger class hierarchy in logging_utils
   - GuidanceTracker dataclass in structure_video_guidance.py

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **source/logging_utils.py** (904 LOC, 18 importers - highest priority)
     - Plan: Split into source/logging/ with core.py, component.py, timing.py, database.py, tasks.py, safe.py

     - Lines 216-246 = LogTimer context manager
     - Lines 248-526 = LogBuffer, WorkerDatabaseLogHandler, CustomLogInterceptor, interceptor wrappers that redefine essential/success/warning/error/debug
     - Lines 528-905 = Safe logging utilities (LOG_MAX_* constants, safe_repr, safe_dict_repr, safe_log_params, safe_json_repr, safe_log_change, SafeComponentLogger, pre-configured safe loggers, docstring)
     - Key complexity: The interceptor section (lines 492-526) redefines module-level functions using `_original_*` pattern - these redefined functions AND the originals must stay in the same module or the interceptor pattern breaks
     - Global state: `_debug_mode`, `_log_file`, `_log_file_lock`, `_log_interceptor`

2. Key Technical Concepts:

   - **Interceptor pattern preservation**: Lines 491-526 of logging_utils.py use `_original_essential = essential` then redefine `essential` - these MUST stay in same module (core.py)
   - **Wan2GP path references**: Changed from `Path(__file__).parent.parent` to `Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent` in decomposed subpackage files
   - **Relative import fix**: vlm_utils had `from .common_utils import ...` which became `from source.common_utils import ...` in new location

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **File decomposition pattern**: Split monolithic file into subpackage with `__init__.py` + multiple focused modules, leave original as thin re-export facade
   - **Re-export facade pattern**: `from source.new_package import *  # noqa: F401,F403`
   - **Circular import avoidance**: When `task_queue/__init__.py` imports from `task_queue.py` and `task_queue.py` imports from the package, fix by having `.py` import from submodules directly, not the package `__init__`
   - **`__file__` path correction**: When moving files deeper into subpackages, `Path(__file__).parent` changes depth. E.g., project root was `.parent` but becomes `.parent.parent.parent` after moving 2 levels deep
   - **Relative to absolute import conversion**: When moving files from `source/` to `source/task_handlers/travel/`, `from .common_utils import ...` must become `from source.common_utils import ...`
   - **Desloppify**: Codebase health scanner tool, run with `desloppify --lang python --exclude Wan2GP --exclude .tmp_kijai_wanvideowrapper --exclude upgrade_patches scan --path .`

Fixed by changing `task_queue.py` to import directly from submodules (`from source.task_queue.download_ops import ...`, `from source.task_queue.worker_thread import ...`, `from source.task_queue.queue_lifecycle import ...`) instead of through the package `__init__`.

   - **`__file__` path corrections**: When moving files to deeper locations, `Path(__file__).parent` needs additional `.parent` calls. Fixed in wgp/orchestrator.py (added `.parent.parent.parent` for samples dir) and debug/cli.py (added `.parent.parent` for project root).

## Overzealousness Audit Results

### CRITICAL: PostgREST `APIError` not caught in `source/db/`
When we narrowed `except Exception` to `except (RuntimeError, ValueError, OSError)` in the db package, we missed that Supabase's PostgREST raises `postgrest.exceptions.APIError` which inherits directly from `Exception` — not from any of those types. This affects ~40 exception handlers across `task_polling.py`, `task_completion.py`, `task_claim.py`, and `task_dependencies.py`.

### Minor: Premature constant extraction
- `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS` in `source/constants.py` is used exactly once — premature abstraction

### Minor: Over-decomposition
- `join/vace_quantization.py` (57 LOC, 1 function) — too small, should merge into `generation.py`
- `travel/ffmpeg_fallback.py` (117 LOC, 1 function) — too small, should merge into `stitch.py`

### Good decisions confirmed
- Re-export facades are necessary (19 files import from them)
- Larger decompositions (db/, task_queue/, wgp/, video/) were well-executed

### 1. **VLM ENHANCEMENT PATTERNS** (CRITICAL - User specifically noted this)

- `/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/join/vlm_enhancement.py` - Join-specific VLM logic
- `/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py` - Travel orchestrator VLM usage

**Duplicated patterns:**

1. **VLM Cleanup Logic** (appears in 3+ places):
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/vlm/prompt_generation.py`: Lines 299-324 (batch cleanup), 360-378 (single image cleanup)
   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/Headless-Wan2GP/source/task_handlers/join/vlm_enhancement.py`: Lines 300-324 (cleanup block)
   - Pattern: Delete model/processor, call gc.collect(), empty cuda cache, measure GPU memory
   - **Consolidation opportunity:** Extract into `source/vlm/cleanup.py` with `_cleanup_vlm_model(extender, device, dprint)` function

2. **Image Pair/Frame Combination**:
   - `vlm/prompt_generation.py`: Lines 232-250, 250-269 (create_framed_vlm_image)
   - `task_handlers/join/vlm_enhancement.py`: Lines 169-178 (manual image combining)
   - Both combine left/right or 4-image sequences side by side
   - **Consolidation opportunity:** Use `create_framed_vlm_image()` consistently or enhance it for both use cases

3. **VLM Model Loading/Download** (appears 4 times):
   - `vlm/prompt_generation.py`: Lines 66-67, 175-176, 422-425, 540-543
   - `task_handlers/join/vlm_enhancement.py`: Lines 265-267
   - `task_handlers/travel/orchestrator.py`: Lines 1166-1168
   - Each loads Wan2GP path, imports QwenPromptExpander, downloads model
   - **Consolidation opportunity:** Create `_initialize_vlm_model(wan_dir, device, dprint)` helper

4. **Batch Processing Structure**:
   - `generate_transition_prompts_batch()` (lines 129-387) - handles pair batching
   - `generate_single_image_prompts_batch()` (lines 500-642) - handles image batching
   - `_generate_vlm_prompts_for_joins()` (lines 211-330 in vlm_enhancement.py) - similar batch loop
   - All have near-identical: for loop, try/except per item, GPU mem tracking

   - **Consolidation opportunity:** Move to `source/constants.py` or `source/vlm/prompts.py`

### 2. **FRAME EXTRACTION PATTERNS**

**Files involved:**

**Duplicated logic:**

1. **Boundary Frame Extraction**:

   - `frame_extraction.py`: Has general extraction but not optimized for boundaries
   - **Consolidation opportunity:** Add `extract_boundary_frames(video_path, first=True, last=True)` to frame_extraction.py

2. **cv2 File I/O Pattern** (appears 8 times):

   - OR: `Image.fromarray(frame_rgb).save()` (PIL approach)

## Comprehensive Source Directory Structure Analysis

### Current Structure Summary

|---------|-------|-------|---------|----------|
| source/db | 7 | 2.3K | Supabase task lifecycle, polling, deps | Stable |
| source/utils | 10 | 2.4K | Prompts, resolution, frames, masks, download | Stable |
| source/params | 10 | 1.8K | Typed dataclasses (TaskConfig, LoRA, etc.) | Stable |
| source/video | 10 | 3.1K | FFmpeg ops, extraction, transforms, travel guide | Stable |
| source/task_handlers | 10 + 2 sub | 1.5K | 8 top-level task handlers (joins, travel) | Mature |
| source/wgp | 8 | 2.7K | WGP orchestration, param resolution, lora | Stable |
| source/worker | 4 | 0.65K | Heartbeat, fatal errors, worker utilities | Stable |
| source/tasks | 4 | 1.8K | Task registry, conversion, types | Core |
| source/lora | 3 | 0.3K | LoRA paths, utilities | Small |
| source/model_handlers | 2 | 0.8K | Qwen handler (model-specific logic) | Growing |
| source/comfy | 3 | 0.4K | ComfyUI integration handler | Small |
| source/structure | 7 | 1.9K | Structure guidance video tracking | Specialized |
| source/visualization | 4 | 0.97K | Debug visualization (timeline, layout, comparison) | Specialized |
| source/vlm | 4 | 0.87K | Vision-language model utilities | Specialized |
| source/log | 6 | 0.85K | Centralized logging (task, db, timing) | Core |
| source/task_queue | 5 | 1.6K | Task queue orchestration, worker loop | Core |

---

## Dependency Analysis

### Critical Patterns Identified

**1. Horizontal Dependencies (Tightly Coupled)**
- `task_handlers` heavily imports from `video`, `utils`, `db`, `params`
- `task_handlers/join` sub-package imports from `task_handlers/join` submodules
- `task_handlers/travel` sub-package similarly self-contained
- `wgp` is mostly self-contained but depends on `logging_utils`, `constants`

**2. Vertical Dependencies (Upward Flows)**
- High-level orchestrators (`tasks`, `task_queue`, `worker`) pull from implementation packages
- Task registry is a hub: imports from `task_handlers`, `tasks`, `lora`, `params`, `model_handlers`, `comfy`
- WGP is independent; called by task_queue

**3. Cross-Cutting Concerns**
- **Logging**: 13+ packages depend on `logging_utils` (now `source/log`)
- **Constants**: 4 packages (`structure`, `vlm`, `db`, `task_queue`) depend on `constants.py`
- **Facades**: 7 backward-compat re-export files in source root (`common_utils.py`, `db_operations.py`, etc.)

**4. Specialized Clusters**
- **Media generation**: `video` + `visualization` (independent, used by handlers)
- **Guidance systems**: `structure` (Uni3C) + `vlm` (vision-language model prep)
- **Task orchestration**: `task_handlers` + `tasks` + `task_queue` (tightly coupled)
- **Model operations**: `wgp` + `model_handlers` + `comfy` (separate concerns)

---

## Proposed Reorganization

### RECOMMENDATION: **Three-Layer Hierarchy with 5 Tiers**

The codebase naturally clusters into **5 distinct domains** that would benefit from explicit organizational tiers:

```
source/
├── core/                          # Infrastructure & Configuration (Layer 0)
│   ├── constants.py               # Byte constants, file extensions
│   ├── db/                        # Supabase task lifecycle
│   ├── log/                       # Centralized logging system
│   ├── params/                    # Typed parameter dataclasses
│   └── platform_utils.py          # Headless environment setup
│
├── utilities/                     # Common Reusable Utilities (Layer 1)
│   ├── utils/                     # Frame, mask, download, resolution
│   ├── video/                     # FFmpeg ops, transforms, extraction
│   ├── lora/                      # LoRA paths & validation
│   └── uni3c_validation.py        # Standalone validation utility
│
├── generation/                    # AI Model & Generation (Layer 2)
│   ├── wgp/                       # WGP orchestration & param resolution
│   ├── model_handlers/            # Model-specific logic (Qwen, etc.)
│   ├── comfy/                     # ComfyUI integration
│   ├── structure/                 # Structure guidance (Uni3C tracking)
│   └── vlm/                       # Vision-language model prep
│
├── tasks/                         # Task Execution & Orchestration (Layer 3)
│   ├── task_queue/                # Task queue, worker thread, lifecycle
│   ├── task_handlers/             # 8 specific task handlers
│   │   ├── join/                  # Join clips orchestration
│   │   └── travel/                # Travel between images orchestration
│   ├── tasks/                     # Registry, conversion, types
│   ├── worker/                    # Heartbeat, fatal errors
│   └── worker_management.py       # (new) High-level worker coordination
│
└── output/                        # Output & Visualization (Layer 4)
    ├── visualization/             # Debug visualizations
    └── specialized_handlers.py    # Extract frame, RIFE interpolate handlers
```

### Why This Structure?

**1. Clear Dependency Flow (Acyclic)**
```
Layer 0: core/
  ↓ (imports only stdlib + third-party)

Layer 1: utilities/
  ↓ (imports Layer 0, no inter-dependencies)

Layer 2: generation/
  ↓ (imports Layer 0-1, mostly self-contained)

Layer 3: tasks/
  ↓ (imports Layer 0-2, orchestrates everything)

Layer 4: output/
  (imports Layer 0-3, display/export only)
```

**2. Domain Isolation**
- **core/** = system initialization (constants, DB, logging, config)
- **utilities/** = stateless transformation functions (image, video, download)
- **generation/** = external service calls (WGP, VLM, ComfyUI, Uni3C)
- **tasks/** = business logic (task lifecycle, workers, scheduling)
- **output/** = result presentation (visualization, handlers)

**3. Import Complexity Reduction**
- Current: 16 packages, horizontal cross-dependencies
- Proposed: 5 tiers, strict vertical-only imports
- **Facade files become unnecessary** (drop 7 root-level re-exports)

---

## Alternative Approaches Evaluated

### Option A: Domain-Driven Design (Rejected)
```
source/
├── task_execution/
├── video_generation/
├── data_layer/
└── presentation/
```
**Problem**: Unclear where `vlm`, `structure`, `comfy` fit; `task_handlers` straddles multiple domains.

### Option B: Flat Monolithic (Current State, Issues)
- 16 packages at same level
- Facades masking real structure
- No guidance for new developers where to add code
- Circular dependencies across `task_handlers` ↔ `utils` ↔ `wgp`

### Option C: By Technology (Rejected)
```
source/
├── ffmpeg/
├── pytorch/
├── comfyui/
└── supabase/
```
**Problem**: Mixes infrastructure (how) with business logic (what).

### Option D: By Task Type (Rejected - Current task_handlers)
- Creates explosion of subdirectories for each task
- Tight coupling between related tasks (join/travel already share logic)
- Hard to refactor common utilities

---

## Migration Path & Risk Assessment

### Phase 1: Create New Tier Structure (Low Risk)
```bash
mkdir -p source/{core,utilities,generation,tasks,output}
# Don't move anything yet, just create dirs
```

### Phase 2: Create New Package __init__.py Files (Zero Risk)
```python
# source/core/__init__.py
from source.log import *
from source.db import *
# etc.
```

### Phase 3: Soft Move with Import Shim (Medium Risk)
```python
# source/constants.py (old location)
from source.core.constants import *  # Re-export from new location
```

### Phase 4: Update Internal Imports (High Risk, Requires Testing)
- Update imports in 50+ files
- Run full test suite after each batch
- Use `desloppify` to detect breakage

### Phase 5: Remove Facade Files (Clean Up)
- Delete `source/common_utils.py`, `source/db_operations.py`, etc.
- Direct imports from new tier structure

**Timeline**: 2-3 weeks with full regression testing

---

## Specific Recommendations

### Immediate Actions (Low Friction)

1. **Rename existing folders for clarity:**
   ```
   source/log/ → source/core/logging/
   source/db/ → source/core/database/
   source/params/ → source/core/config/
   ```

2. **Create explicit entry points:**
   ```python
   # source/core/__init__.py (new)
   """Core infrastructure: logging, DB, config, constants."""
   from source.core.logging import *  # noqa: F401,F403
   from source.core.database import *  # noqa: F401,F403
   from source.core.config import *  # noqa: F401,F403
   ```

3. **Consolidate loose files:**
   ```
   Move source/platform_utils.py → source/core/platform.py
   Move source/constants.py → source/core/constants.py
   Move source/uni3c_validation.py → source/utilities/validation.py (standalone)
   ```

4. **Clarify facade purpose:**
   - `common_utils.py` (↔ source/utilities/)
   - `video_utils.py` (↔ source/utilities/video/)
   - `logging_utils.py` (↔ source/core/logging/)
   - Add `__all__` to each facade to be explicit

### Medium-Term Refactoring (Higher Value)

1. **Split `task_handlers` into tiers:**
   ```
   tasks/orchestration/
   ├── join_clips_orchestrator.py
   ├── edit_video_orchestrator.py
   └── travel_between_images.py (entry point re-export)
   
   tasks/implementations/
   ├── join/
   ├── travel/
   ├── inpaint_frames.py
   ├── magic_edit.py
   └── create_visualization.py
   ```
   **Benefit**: Clear orchestrator vs. implementation boundary

2. **Extract `worker_management` from root:**
   ```
   source/tasks/worker_management/
   ├── __init__.py
   ├── heartbeat.py (← source/worker/heartbeat_utils.py)
   ├── error_handling.py (← source/worker/fatal_error_handler.py)
   ├── lifecycle.py (← source/task_queue/queue_lifecycle.py)
   └── polling.py (← source/db/task_polling.py)
   ```

3. **Merge related packages:**
   - `source/vlm/ + source/visualization/` → `source/generation/media/` (both about output creation)
   - OR keep separate (visualization is debug-only, vlm is critical path)

### Do NOT Do (Anti-Patterns)

- **Don't create `utils/utils/` recursion** (already have `utils.` → limit to one level)
- **Don't split by file size** (split by domain/coupling)
- **Don't merge unrelated packages just for cleanliness** (`comfy` ≠ `vlm`, even though both small)
- **Don't lose backward-compat imports yet** (keep facades during transition)

---

## Code Health Metrics to Track

After reorganization, monitor these:

| Metric | Current | Target | Tool |
|--------|---------|--------|------|
| Max import depth | 4 layers | 2 layers | desloppify |
| Circular imports | ~3 detected | 0 | `python -m py_compile` |
| Cross-package imports | 47% | 25% | desloppify --query-output |
| Facade usage | 7 files | 0 files | grep for re-exports |
| Avg package coupling | High | Low | desloppify |

---

## Summary Table: Before vs. After

| Dimension | Before | After |
|-----------|--------|-------|
| **Top-level packages** | 16 flat | 5 tiers (5-10 packages each) |
| **Facade files** | 7 re-exports | 5 (1 per tier) |
| **Import rule clarity** | Implicit | Explicit (layer-based) |
| **Onboarding difficulty** | High | Low |
| **Circular dependencies** | ~3-4 detected | 0 (enforced) |
| **Test time (est.)** | N/A | +10% (initially) |
| **Maintenance friction** | Medium | Low |

---

## Recommended Immediate Next Step

**Create `source/core/` package with consolidated infrastructure:**

```python
# source/core/__init__.py (NEW)
"""Core infrastructure: logging, database, config, constants."""

from source.core.logging import *  # noqa: F401,F403
from source.core.database import *  # noqa: F401,F403  
from source.core.config import *  # noqa: F401,F403
from source.core.constants import *  # noqa: F401,F403

    # Logging
    'headless_logger', 'task_logger', 'model_logger', 'generation_logger', 'queue_logger',
    # Database
    'claim_task', 'complete_task', 'fail_task', 'get_task_dependencies',
    # Config
    'TaskConfig', 'LoRAConfig', 'PhaseConfig', 'StructureGuidanceConfig',
    # Constants
    'BYTES_PER_KB', 'BYTES_PER_MB', 'BYTES_PER_GB', 'VIDEO_EXTENSIONS',
]
```

This gives you:
- A clear "infrastructure" tier
- Single import path for system initialization
- Foundation for further reorganization
- No breaking changes (soft migration)

---

This analysis reveals a healthy codebase with well-defined packages that would benefit from explicit layering to reduce cognitive load and improve maintainability. The 5-tier hierarchy respects the current decomposition while adding strategic guidance for future growth.</result>

<result>Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive audit report:

## Code Health Audit Report: Desloppify Changes Assessment

I've completed a thorough investigation of the recent desloppify-driven changes across the codebase. Below is my detailed findings with specific recommendations.

---

## CRITICAL ISSUES

### 1. **Exception Narrowing: MISSING PostgREST APIError Handling**

**Status: TOO NARROW - CRITICAL BUG**

**What was done:**
- Narrowed `except Exception` to `(RuntimeError, ValueError, OSError)` in the `source/db/` package
- This pattern appears in 40+ locations across `task_polling.py`, `task_completion.py`, `task_claim.py`, and `task_dependencies.py`

**The Problem:**
- PostgREST (used by Supabase) raises `postgrest.exceptions.APIError` when database queries fail
- `APIError` is **NOT a subclass** of `RuntimeError`, `ValueError`, or `OSError` — it's a bare `Exception`
- When `.execute()` is called on Supabase client queries, `APIError` will **NOT be caught** by current handlers
- This allows database errors to propagate unhandled, crashing tasks that should gracefully degrade

**Evidence:**
```python
# source/db/task_polling.py:54-58
resp = _cfg.SUPABASE_CLIENT.table(_cfg.PG_TABLE_NAME).select("status, output_location").eq("id", task_id).single().execute()
# Can raise: postgrest.exceptions.APIError (NOT caught!)

# Current exception handler (too narrow):
except (httpx.HTTPError, OSError, ValueError, KeyError) as e:
    # APIError escapes here
```

**Files affected:**
- `/source/db/task_polling.py` - 4 exception handlers
- `/source/db/task_completion.py` - 14 exception handlers  
- `/source/db/task_claim.py` - 8 exception handlers
- `/source/db/task_dependencies.py` - 10+ exception handlers

**Recommendation:** Add `APIError` to all database exception handlers:
```python
from postgrest.exceptions import APIError
except (APIError, httpx.HTTPError, OSError, ValueError, KeyError) as e:
```

**Severity:** CRITICAL - This will cause silent task failures in production

---

### 2. **Exception Narrowing: subprocess.CalledProcessError Not Caught**

**Status: TOO NARROW - MODERATE ISSUE**

**What was done:**
- Narrowed subprocess exception handling to `(subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError)`
- Appears in video operations: `source/video/ffmpeg_ops.py:118`, `source/utils/frame_utils.py:118`, `source/video/video_info.py:70`

**The Problem:**
- `subprocess.CalledProcessError` is a **direct subclass of `subprocess.SubprocessError`**, so this is caught ✓
- However, the catch pattern is explicitly named but...
- The narrowing to just these types may miss `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` in some execution paths
- More importantly, **FileNotFoundError** (when ffmpeg binary is missing) is an `OSError`, so that's covered ✓

**Actually this one is OK** - verified that `CalledProcessError ⊆ SubprocessError` and `TimeoutExpired` is handled explicitly in most places

---

### 3. **Exception Narrowing: JSON Parsing Errors**

**Status: PROBABLY OK BUT INCONSISTENT**

**Pattern observed:**
- `source/db/edge_helpers.py:166` catches `(ValueError, KeyError, json.JSONDecodeError)`
- `source/db/task_dependencies.py:489` catches `(json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError)`

**The Reality:**
- In Python 3.5+, `json.JSONDecodeError` is a subclass of `ValueError`
- So catching `ValueError` alone would catch JSON errors
- Current pattern is defensive/redundant but not wrong

---

## SIGNIFICANT ISSUES (Non-Critical)

### 4. **VIDEO_EXTENSIONS Constant: Premature Abstraction**

**Status: OVERZEALOUS - Minor Negative Impact**

**What was done:**
- Extracted magic numbers and byte constants into `source/constants.py` (lines 1-11)
- Constants include: `BYTES_PER_KB`, `BYTES_PER_MB`, `BYTES_PER_GB`, `MB_PER_GB`, `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS`

**The Problem:**
- `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS` is **used exactly once** in the codebase:
  ```python
  # source/db/task_completion.py
  from source.constants import VIDEO_EXTENSIONS
  is_video = output_path.suffix.lower() in VIDEO_EXTENSIONS
  ```
- Extracting a single-use constant adds cognitive load with no reuse benefit
- The other byte constants ARE reused (48 uses across multiple files) ✓

**Recommendation:** Remove `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS` from constants and define it inline where it's used, or in a video-specific module like `source/video/` package `__init__.py`

**Impact:** Low - just unnecessary imports, doesn't break functionality

---

### 5. **Over-Decomposition: Very Small Modules in join/ and travel/**

**Status: QUESTIONABLE - Minor Readability Concern**

**Files with <100 LOC of logic:**

| Module | Lines | Content | Assessment |
|--------|-------|---------|------------|
| `source/task_handlers/travel/debug_utils.py` | 127 | 2 helper functions for video debug & RAM monitoring | Could stay, useful separation |
| `source/task_handlers/travel/ffmpeg_fallback.py` | 117 | 1 function for FFmpeg crossfade | **Too small - should merge into stitch.py** |
| `source/task_handlers/travel/svi_config.py` | 111 | Constants + 1 function for SVI LoRA arrays | **Could be in __init__.py** |
| `source/task_handlers/join/vace_quantization.py` | 57 | 1 function for VACE frame quantization | **Too small - should merge into generation.py** |

**The Problem:**
- These tiny modules require extra imports and add navigation overhead
- Single-function modules violate the "module should be at least 50 LOC" heuristic
- Indirection: `join_clips.py` imports from `join/vace_quantization.py` just to re-export
- Harder to find related logic when scattered across many files

**Current usage patterns:**
```python
# source/task_handlers/join_clips.py (the old unified file)
from source.task_handlers.join.vace_quantization import _calculate_vace_quantization
from source.task_handlers.join.vace_quantization import *

# source/task_handlers/join/generation.py
from .vace_quantization import _calculate_vace_quantization
```

**Recommendation:** 
- Merge `vace_quantization.py` → `join/generation.py` (where it's actually used)
- Merge `ffmpeg_fallback.py` → `travel/stitch.py` (related functionality)
- Keep `svi_config.py` ✓ (it's a config dict + utility function, reasonable separation)

---

## GOOD DECISIONS ✓

### Re-Export Facades: NECESSARY AND USED

**What was done:**
- Kept facade files like `source/common_utils.py`, `source/db_operations.py`, `source/video_utils.py`
- These just `from source.new_package import *` for backward compatibility

**Verification:**
- **19 files** actively import from facade locations (not just the facades themselves)
- Examples:
  - `source/task_queue/worker_thread.py` imports `from source.common_utils import ...`
  - `source/model_handlers/qwen_handler.py` imports `from source.common_utils import ...`
  - `source/db_operations.py` uses special `_ConfigProxy` to intercept writes from `worker.py`

**Assessment:** These are NECESSARY ✓ because:
1. Worker.py sets attributes dynamically: `db_ops.SUPABASE_URL = ...`
2. The `_ConfigProxy` pattern properly propagates these writes to submodules
3. Removing would require refactoring worker.py's initialization

---

### Reasonable Decompositions ✓

**Files that were well-decomposed:**
- `source/db/` package: 6 focused modules (config, task_polling, task_completion, task_claim, task_dependencies, edge_helpers) - **GOOD**
- `source/task_queue/` package: 4 modules with clear responsibilities - **GOOD**
- `source/wgp/` package: 8 modules, well-organized by concern - **GOOD**
- `source/video/` package: 8 modules (ffmpeg_ops, frame_extraction, video_info, etc.) - **GOOD**

---

## SUMMARY OF ISSUES

| Issue | Severity | Action Required |
|-------|----------|-----------------|
| Missing APIError in db exception handlers | **CRITICAL** | Add `APIError` to 40+ exception handlers in source/db/ |
| VIDEO_EXTENSIONS premature abstraction | Low | Remove from constants.py, define inline |
| vace_quantization.py too small | Low | Merge into generation.py |
| ffmpeg_fallback.py too small | Low | Merge into travel/stitch.py |
| svi_config.py small but appropriate | None | Keep as-is |

---

## RECOMMENDATIONS PRIORITY ORDER

### Must Do (Fixes Production Bug):
1. **Add PostgREST APIError handling to all db/ exception handlers**
   ```python
   from postgrest.exceptions import APIError
   # In all except clauses in source/db/:
   except (APIError, httpx.HTTPError, OSError, ValueError, KeyError) as e:
   ```

### Should Do (Code Quality):
2. Merge `source/task_handlers/join/vace_quantization.py` → `join/generation.py`
3. Merge `source/task_handlers/travel/ffmpeg_fallback.py` → `travel/stitch.py`
4. Remove `VIDEO_EXTENSIONS` from `source/constants.py`

### Nice to Have:
5. Consider consolidating `svi_config.py` constants into `travel/__init__.py` (but current structure is acceptable)

---

## CONCLUSION

The desloppify refactoring was **mostly well-executed**, but one critical oversight was discovered: **PostgREST APIError exceptions are not being caught**, which will cause unhandled database errors in production. This needs immediate attention.

The over-decomposition issues are minor and mostly impact code navigation rather than functionality, but the code would be cleaner if some <100 LOC modules were merged with their related siblings.</result>

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is executing a comprehensive structural cleanup of the Headless-Wan2GP project. This session continued from a previous session where tasks #1-#11 (decompositions, verifications) were completed. The current session focused on:

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **PostgREST APIError**: `postgrest.exceptions.APIError` inherits directly from `Exception`, not from standard types like `RuntimeError`/`ValueError`/`OSError`. This means narrowed exception handlers miss it.
   - **Python `import *` and underscore convention**: `from module import *` silently skips names starting with `_` unless `__all__` is defined in the module. This caused facade re-exports to silently drop functions.
   - **desloppify move command**: Moves files and updates all import references. Has 3 bugs:
     - `finalize_graph` uses substring matching on absolute paths for exclusions — `"Wan2GP"` in exclude list matches project root `Headless-Wan2GP`, excluding ALL files from the dependency graph
     - `_has_exact_module` uses `\b` word boundary which doesn't work with dots in module paths
     - Only supports file moves, not directory/package moves
   - **desloppify dependency graph building**: Uses grep to find all import/from statements, builds a graph of `{resolved_path: {imports, importers}}`. Files without imports are still added as targets when other files import from them (via defaultdict).
   - **Proposed source/ reorganization**: 17 directories → 5 groups:
     - `core/` ← db/, log/, params/, constants.py, platform_utils.py
     - `media/` ← video/, visualization/, vlm/, structure/
     - `models/` ← wgp/, model_handlers/, comfy/, lora/
     - `task_handlers/` ← existing + tasks/, task_queue/, worker/
     - `utils/` ← stays as-is

The self-import changes are wrong. When moving the entire `source/db/` directory, internal relative imports within the package (like `from .config`) should stay the same since all files move together. I need to fix this:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
```json
desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py
```

> TOOL

tool_use Read
```json
desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py
```

> AGENT

I need to rethink the directory move approach. The fundamental issue: when moving an entire directory, `_find_py_self_replacements` computes replacements as if each file is being individually moved while its imports stay in place. But in a directory move, all files move together, so intra-package imports should be unchanged.

The correct approach for self-import changes: only update imports that point OUTSIDE the moved directory. For intra-package imports, they should stay the same.

For importer changes: only files OUTSIDE the moved directory should be updated. Files inside the directory that import each other don't need changes.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
```json
desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py
```

Now let me address all your questions:

**1. Should the `__init__.py` / intra-package absolute import fix be part of the move command?**

Yes, absolutely. When `desloppify move` moves a directory, it should rewrite ALL imports referencing the old location — including absolute imports inside the package itself (like `from source.video.ffmpeg_ops` inside `source/video/crossfade.py`). Currently it only handles:
- External importers (files outside the package)
- Self relative imports (`from .foo`)

It misses intra-package absolute imports, which is what I had to fix manually. I'll update the desloppify PR.

**2. `unittest.mock.patch` string paths** — also a move command limitation. These are string literals, not actual imports. Desloppify can't easily detect those (they'd need AST parsing for `patch()` calls), but it's worth noting in the docs.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is executing a comprehensive structural cleanup of the Headless-Wan2GP project. This session's primary requests were:
   - Clone desloppify, fix its `move` command bugs (broken import detection, no directory support), implement directory move support, and create a PR on the original repo
   - Use the fixed desloppify to reorganize source/ from 17 subdirectories into 5 top-level groups (core/, media/, models/, task_handlers/, utils/)
   - Make the tool completely bug-free with --dry-run testing before applying
   - Fix any issues discovered with the move command (intra-package absolute imports) and update the PR
   - Create tasks for remaining cleanup work
   - Continue with all remaining tasks (#22-#25): clean up loose facade files, consolidate common_utils, update __init__.py, run verification

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Desloppify move command**: Moves files/directories and updates all import references. Has bugs with exclusion matching, regex for dotted modules, missing absolute from-imports in dep graph, and missing intra-package absolute import rewriting during directory moves.
   - **Exclusion matching on absolute vs relative paths**: `--exclude Wan2GP` matched every file when project root was `Headless-Wan2GP` because substring matching was done on absolute paths.
   - **Python import graph building**: Uses grep to find all import/from statements, resolves to files. The `from_re` regex only matched relative imports, missing all absolute `from X import Y`.
   - **`\b` word boundary vs lookaround**: `\b` treats `.` as a word boundary, so `source.foo` falsely matches `source.foo.bar`. Fixed with `(?<!\w)` and `(?![\w.])`.
   - **Directory move semantics**: When moving a package, intra-package relative imports stay the same (files move together), but intra-package absolute imports AND external importers need rewriting.
   - **Re-export facades**: Thin .py files in source/ root that `from source.subpackage import *` for backward compatibility.
   - **`_ConfigProxy` antipattern**: `db_operations.py` intercepts attribute writes to propagate config to sub-modules.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   **Desloppify changes (5 files modified):**

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/detectors/graph.py`
     - Fixed exclusion matching to use relative paths
     ```python
     from pathlib import Path
     from ..utils import rel, resolve_path, PROJECT_ROOT
     
     # In finalize_graph():
     excluded_keys = set()
     for k in graph:
         try:
             rel_k = str(Path(k).relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT))
         except ValueError:
             rel_k = k
         if any(ex in rel_k for ex in _extra_exclusions):
             excluded_keys.add(k)
     ```

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/utils.py`
     - Fixed `run_grep` to match exclusions against relative file paths only
     ```python
     def run_grep(cmd: list[str]) -> str:
         result = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=PROJECT_ROOT)
         if not _extra_exclusions or not result.stdout:
             return result.stdout
         lines = result.stdout.splitlines()
         filtered = []
         for line in lines:
             colon_idx = line.find(":")
             file_part = line[:colon_idx] if colon_idx != -1 else line
             try:
                 file_part = str(Path(file_part).relative_to(PROJECT_ROOT))
             except ValueError:
                 pass
             if not any(ex in file_part for ex in _extra_exclusions):
                 filtered.append(line)
         return "\n".join(filtered) + ("\n" if filtered else "")
     ```

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/lang/python/detectors/deps.py`
     - Fixed `from_re` regex to match both relative and absolute from-imports
     ```python
     # Match both relative (from .foo import bar) and absolute (from foo import bar)
     from_re = re.compile(r"^from\s+(\.+\w*(?:\.\w+)*|\w+(?:\.\w+)*)\s+import\s+(.+)")
     ```

   - `/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/commands/move.py`
     - Fixed `_has_exact_module` and `_replace_exact_module` regex
     ```python
     def _has_exact_module(line: str, module: str) -> bool:
         return bool(re.search(rf'(?<!\w){re.escape(module)}(?![\w.])', line))
     
     def _replace_exact_module(line: str, old_module: str, new_module: str) -> str:
         return re.sub(rf'(?<!\w){re.escape(old_module)}(?![\w.])', new_module, line)
     ```

Fixed by adding `intra_pkg_changes` dict that tracks absolute imports between co-moving files, filtering out relative imports with `re.match(r"from\s+\.", old)`.

Now I understand the gap. Let me investigate the desloppify detectors more precisely and then create the issue.

The three deleted facades (`visualization_utils.py`, `specialized_handlers.py`, `travel_segment_processor.py`) were all 2-3 line re-export files like:
```python
from source.media.visualization import *  # noqa: F401,F403
```

Here's why desloppify missed them:
- **Orphaned detector**: They weren't orphaned — they had importers (even if just `__init__.py` or `task_registry.py`)
- **Passthrough detector**: Only detects passthrough _functions_, not re-export _files_
- **Single-use detector**: Would catch them, but they weren't single-use in a meaningful sense — they're facades
- **No "thin re-export facade" detector** exists

Let me file the issue:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
```json
cd /user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify && gh issue create --title "Missing detector: thin re-export facade files" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Problem

After decomposing large files into packages (e.g., `source/visualization_utils.py` → `source/media/visualization/`), backward-compatible re-export 
```

> AGENT

Filed issue [#6](https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify/issues/6). The gap is that desloppify has no detector for "thin re-export facade" files — files whose entire content is just `from X import *` with no original code. The orphaned detector misses them because they have importers, and the passthrough detector only looks at functions, not files.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is executing a comprehensive structural cleanup of the Headless-Wan2GP project. This session continued from prior work and covered:
   - Clean up loose facade files in source/ (delete unnecessary ones, update importers)
   - File an issue on desloppify for why it missed thin facade files (#6)
   - Update the issue to also cover "thin facade folders" (packages with re-export-only __init__.py)
   - Consolidate common_utils.py by updating all 33 importers (15 absolute + 18 relative) to use `source.utils` directly
   - Eliminate remaining facade files (video_utils.py, logging_utils.py, vlm_utils.py, structure_video_guidance.py importers)
   - "Keep searching for how this code could be more beautiful, the bar is beautiful"
   - Fix all stale comments, docstrings, and development phase markers
   - Push everything to a branch
   - Continue auditing for anything else that can be cleaned up or moved
   - User asked why debug.py and debug/ folder both exist
   - User asked if structure_video_guidance.py should be in Wan2GP instead

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Re-export facade files**: `.py` files whose only content is `from X import *`, serving as backward-compat shims
   - **Re-export facade packages**: `__init__.py` files that only contain `from .submodule import *` re-exports
   - **Dual-layer facades**: A facade file re-exporting from a facade package (two layers of indirection)
   - **_ConfigProxy pattern**: `source/db_operations.py` uses a module-wrapping class that intercepts `setattr` to propagate runtime config writes to submodules
   - **Vendored code sys.path hack**: `Wan2GP/models/wan/any2video.py` adds `source/` to sys.path and does `from structure_video_guidance import ...` — requires the facade to physically exist in `source/`
   - **Relative import dot counting**: `...` goes up 3 package levels; changing `from ...common_utils` to `from ...utils` when the facade is eliminated
   - **mock.patch path targets**: Must match the module where the name is looked up, not where it's defined
   - **desloppify detectors**: orphaned, passthrough (functions only), single-use — none detect thin re-export facades

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`source/__init__.py`** — Package init with deferred-load imports for static analysis. Modified multiple times to remove facade references. Final state:
     ```python
     # Explicit imports for modules used via deferred/dynamic loading.
     from source.models.wgp import wgp_patches  # noqa: F401
     from source.media.video import vace_frame_utils  # noqa: F401
     from source.media.video import hires_utils  # noqa: F401
     from source.models.lora import lora_utils  # noqa: F401
     from source.task_handlers.worker import heartbeat_utils  # noqa: F401
     from source.task_handlers.worker import fatal_error_handler  # noqa: F401
     ```

   - **`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`** — Central task dispatch. Updated imports from facades to real modules:
     - `from source.specialized_handlers import (...)` → `from source.task_handlers.extract_frame import handle_extract_frame_task` + `from source.task_handlers.rife_interpolate import handle_rife_interpolate_task`
     - `from source.travel_segment_processor import TravelSegmentProcessor, TravelSegmentContext` → `from source.task_handlers.travel.segment_processor import ...`
     - `from source.common_utils import (...)` → `from source.utils import (...)`
     - `from source.video_utils import extract_last_frame_as_image` → `from source.media.video import extract_last_frame_as_image`
     - `from source.logging_utils import headless_logger` → `from source.core.log import headless_logger`

   - **Deleted facade files** (7 total this session):
     - `source/visualization_utils.py` (0 importers)
     - `source/specialized_handlers.py` (1 importer: task_registry.py)
     - `source/travel_segment_processor.py` (1 importer: task_registry.py)

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user is executing a comprehensive structural cleanup of the Headless-Wan2GP project. This session continued from TWO previous conversations. The specific requests this session were:
   - "is the holistically elegant now?" — requesting a final audit of the codebase
   - "Can you figure out how to deal with those 3 problems please?" — referring to: (1) _ConfigProxy antipattern in db_operations.py, (2) print-to-logger standardization (~1,300 calls), (3) 3 pre-existing test failures
   - "WE could add an exception for the failing tests - when on a machine where they couldn't pass" — guidance on test fix approach
   - When asked about print-to-logger approach, user chose "Do it all now" — convert all ~1,300 print calls to logger calls

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Path(__file__) parent traversal**: Files at `source/X/Y/Z.py` (depth 4) need `.parent.parent.parent.parent` to reach project root. After reorganization, many files had incorrect parent counts.
   - **_ConfigProxy pattern**: Module-level wrapper (`sys.modules[__name__] = _ConfigProxy(...)`) that intercepts `__setattr__` to propagate config writes from `db_operations` facade to `source.core.db.config`. Eliminated by having writer (worker.py) write directly to config module.
   - **Star import binding**: `from module import *` copies values at import time. Later mutations to the source module don't propagate — this is why the proxy existed.
   - **dprint pattern**: Functions receive `dprint` as a parameter closure that captures `task_id` and `debug_mode`. Internally `dprint` already routes through `headless_logger.debug()`. Raw `print()` calls bypass the logger entirely.
   - **pytest 8.x**: Rejects test functions that return non-None values (must use `assert` instead of `return True`).
   - **Re-export facades**: `.py` files whose only content is `from X import *`, serving as backward-compat shims.
   - **Vendored code**: `Wan2GP/` directory contains modified third-party code that `sys.path`-manipulates to import from `source/`.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_main.py`** (lines 17-18)
     - Fixed broken vendored imports after reorganization
     - `from source.params.phase_multiplier_utils` → `from source.core.params.phase_multiplier_utils`
     - `from source.video.hires_utils` → `from source.media.video.hires_utils`

   - **`Wan2GP/models/z_image/z_image_main.py`** (line 22)
     - Same hires_utils fix: `from source.video.hires_utils` → `from source.media.video.hires_utils`

   - **`debug.py`** — Deleted (redundant with `debug/__main__.py`)

   - **`create_test_task.py`** — Moved to `scripts/create_test_task.py`

   - **`source/task_handlers/join_clips.py`** — Deleted (facade)
   - **`source/task_handlers/join_clips_orchestrator.py`** — Deleted (facade)
   - **`source/task_handlers/travel_between_images.py`** — Deleted (facade)

   - **`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_registry.py`** (lines 28-34)
     - Updated from facade imports to real module imports:
     ```python
     from source.task_handlers.travel import orchestrator as travel_orchestrator
     from source.task_handlers.travel.stitch import _handle_travel_stitch_task
     from source.task_handlers import magic_edit as me
     from source.task_handlers.join.generation import _handle_join_clips_task
     ```

Good findings. Now on the desloppify question — here's the answer:

**Why desloppify didn't flag `.tmp_kijai_wanvideowrapper`:** Because we told it not to! We pass `--exclude .tmp_kijai_wanvideowrapper` on every scan. Excluded directories are invisible to all detectors. This is a chicken-and-egg problem: you exclude directories to avoid false positives from vendored code, but then you also hide genuinely dead directories.

**Is this worth reporting?** Yes — a useful feature would be an **"excluded directory audit"** that checks whether excluded paths are actually referenced from non-excluded code. If nothing imports from or references an excluded directory, it should flag it as "potentially stale exclude." I'll file it.

Here are the deletable candidates found:

| `agent_docs/` | 176 KB | 0 | AI agent planning docs for completed features (Uni3C, Qwen, etc.) |
| `docs/` | 52 KB | 0 | SVI implementation reference docs |

`docs/` I'd keep — it's small and serves as developer reference. The other three are all git-recoverable backups/planning artifacts from completed work.

This is **not from our changes** — it's a pre-existing issue. The flow is:

1. `--reigh-access-token` → stored as `access_token` (line 233) → used for API auth (line 278)
2. The Supabase **client** needs a separate key: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` env var, `--supabase-anon-key`, or `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` env var (line 269)

On their RunPod deployment, the `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` env var would normally be set in the container environment. If it's missing, the worker can't init the database client.

The worker on that machine needs either:
- `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` set as an env var, **or**
- `--supabase-anon-key <key>` passed on the CLI

The `--reigh-access-token` alone isn't enough — it's for Reigh API authentication, not for the Supabase database connection. This has always been the case; nothing we changed touches this path. You can verify by checking `git log --oneline -5 -- worker.py` on that machine — the Supabase init block (lines 266-287) hasn't been modified by our refactor.

> AGENT

Found it. The old `db_operations.py` had this at line 497-499:

```python
if not worker_id:
    worker_id = "gpu-20250723_221138-afa8403b"  # hardcoded default
```

That was a hardcoded test worker ID that papered over the missing `--worker` flag. It got removed during our refactor (correctly — it was terrible). But nothing replaced it.

My hostname fix is actually the right approach for local workers. Here's why it's safe:

- **Local workers** (your case): hostname gives a stable, unique ID per machine. Each user's machine gets its own identity.
- **RunPod workers**: they already pass `--worker` explicitly, so the auto-derive never triggers.
- **The old hardcoded ID was worse**: every local user would share the same worker_id `gpu-20250723_221138-afa8403b`, stepping on each other's tasks.

The only place `worker_id` is used downstream is task claiming and recovery (`check_my_assigned_tasks`), which are both fine with a hostname-based ID. It won't cause issues elsewhere.

The old code literally had this hardcoded fallback in `db_operations.py`:

```python
if not worker_id:
    worker_id = "gpu-20250723_221138-afa8403b"
```

That means **every** local user was pretending to be the same worker. It "worked" because there was probably only ever one local user at a time — the edge function just grabs the next queued task and assigns it to whatever `worker_id` claims it.

The flow is:
1. User creates task in Reigh app → task goes into queue as "Queued"
2. Worker polls `claim-next-task` edge function with `{"worker_id": "...", "run_type": "gpu"}`
3. Edge function assigns next queued task to that worker_id
4. Worker processes it

The worker_id just needs to be *something* — it's how the system tracks which worker owns a task. For local users, hostname is actually better than the old hardcoded string because two different local users won't collide.

The hostname approach I pushed does exactly what the old code did, but without the hardcoded hack. If someone runs `python worker.py --reigh-access-token <token>` on their laptop, their hostname (e.g. `Peters-MacBook-Pro.local`) becomes their worker_id. Tasks get claimed and processed the same way.

> AGENT

You're right, that's simpler and cleaner. The hostname can be ugly (`a86e8120f181`, `Peters-MacBook-Pro.local`) and doesn't add real value over a generic name.

Pushed. Now about the actual error — the debug output shows exactly what's happening:

**Task counts work fine** — 8 queued tasks found. The problem is the **claim edge function** returning 500:

```
Could not choose the best candidate function between: 
  public.claim_next_task_user_pat(p_user_id => uuid, p_include_active => boolean), 
  public.claim_next_task_user_pat(p_user_id => uuid, p_include_active => boolean, p_run_type => text)
```

This is a **server-side PostgreSQL function overload ambiguity**. There are two versions of `claim_next_task_user_pat` in the database — one with 2 params and one with 3 — and PostgREST can't figure out which one to call.

**This isn't something we broke** — it's a Supabase/database issue. The fix needs to happen in the edge function or the database:
1. Drop the old 2-parameter overload, or
2. Have the edge function explicitly call the 3-parameter version with a cast, or
3. Rename one of them

The logging fix I just pushed means you'll now see `[CLAIM] Edge Function returned 500: ...` visibly even without `--debug`, so this failure won't be silent anymore.

So this is **not caused by our refactor**. The error is a server-side PostgreSQL function overload issue — someone added a second version of `claim_next_task_user_pat` with an extra `p_run_type` parameter without dropping the old 2-parameter version, and now PostgREST can't disambiguate between them.

This would fail the same way on main. Most likely either:
1. The DB function overload was added recently (server-side change), or
2. Local workers using PAT auth (`--reigh-access-token`) haven't been tested against this edge function recently — RunPod workers may use a different auth path that calls a different SQL function (e.g. `claim_next_task` instead of `claim_next_task_user_pat`)

The fix is on the Supabase side: drop the old 2-parameter `claim_next_task_user_pat` overload, or have the edge function explicitly disambiguate.

> AGENT

Two issues jump out:

**1. Task ID is not a UUID** — the `update-task-status` edge function fails:
```
invalid input syntax for type uuid: "qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac"
```
The DB expects UUID task IDs, but this task has a human-readable ID. This means status updates (like `generation_started_at` reset) fail. Is this a test task created locally?

**2. LoRA multipliers 2 and 3 aren't being applied** — The task specifies `loras_multipliers: 0.9,1.1,0.5` but the actual load shows:
```
[LORA_LOAD_CONFIRM]   [0] Lightning  @ multiplier=0.9   ← correct
[LORA_LOAD_CONFIRM]   [1] style_transfer  @ multiplier=1.0   ← should be 1.1
[LORA_LOAD_CONFIRM]   [2] in_subject  @ multiplier=1.0   ← should be 0.5
```
Only the first LoRA gets its correct multiplier; LoRAs 2 and 3 fall back to 1.0. This is consistent with the LORA_APPLICATION_TRACE which shows the entire string `0.9,1.1,0.5` being assigned to LoRA #1, while LoRAs #2 and #3 get `1.0`. WGP isn't correctly parsing the comma-separated multiplier string for Qwen models.

**Minor/non-issues:**
- `⚠️ Current model qwen_image_edit_20B may not be optimized for T2V` — false warning, this is image gen not T2V
- `Failed (non-fatal): qwen_model_routing` — marked as non-fatal debug, likely fine
- Model loading took ~7 min (first download) — expected behavior

The LoRA multiplier bug is the important one — it means style_transfer and subject LoRAs are running at wrong strengths.

Good, the new output confirms both issues. Here's what the subagents found:

## Issue 1: Task ID (server-side)

The `claim-next-task` edge function is returning `params.task_id` (`qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac`) as the top-level `task_id` instead of the database row UUID (`78dfabdf-...`). Our code at `worker.py:371` uses `task_info["task_id"]`, so all subsequent edge function calls fail with "invalid input syntax for type uuid". **This is a server-side fix** in the edge function.

## Issue 2: LoRA multipliers (our code)

The `LORA_SLISTS_CONFIRM` output makes it crystal clear:

```
phase1 multipliers: [[0.9, 1.1, 0.5], 1.0, 1.0]
```

The entire list `[0.9, 1.1, 0.5]` is assigned as LoRA 1's multiplier (nested list!), while LoRAs 2 and 3 default to `1.0`.

**Root cause:** In `source/wgp/orchestrator.py`, the multipliers are joined as a comma-separated string (`"0.9,1.1,0.5"`), but WGP's `preparse_loras_multipliers()` in `Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py` splits on **spaces/newlines**, not commas. So the whole string becomes one entry.

**Fix:** Change the join separator from `,` to space ` ` in the orchestrator, or pass as a list (WGP handles lists natively at line 5 of `preparse_loras_multipliers`).

## CPU stall (~7 min gap)

The gap from 18:50:06 to 18:56:58 is likely WGP doing prompt encoding/preprocessing on first run. Since it proceeded on its own, this is probably normal first-run behavior rather than a bug.

Found it — it's actually **both** a client-side and server-side issue.

Here's the exact chain:

**Step 1 — Edge function returns wrong `task_id`:**
The debug log shows:
```
[CLAIM] Claimed task qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac (type=qwen_image_style)
```
This comes from `task_claim.py:308`: `task_id = task_data.get('task_id', 'unknown')`. So the `claim-next-task` edge function is returning `params.task_id` (the human-readable ID) as the top-level `task_id`, not the database UUID `78dfabdf-57af-4c78-8bf5-919ac1dc9b82`.

**Step 2 — Worker stores both IDs but uses the wrong one:**
```python
# worker.py:368-371
current_task_params = task_info["params"]    # params dict, contains params.task_id = "qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac"
current_task_id = task_info["task_id"]       # ALSO "qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac" (from edge function)
```

**Step 3 — Only orchestrator types get the fix:**
```python
# worker.py:377-379
if current_task_type in {"travel_orchestrator", "join_clips_orchestrator", ...}:
    current_task_params["task_id"] = current_task_id  # Overwrites params.task_id with edge function's task_id
```
For `qwen_image_style`, this block is **skipped**, so `params["task_id"]` stays as `"qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac"`.

**Step 4 — process_single_task reads from params:**
```python
# worker.py:137
task_id = task_params_dict.get("task_id", "unknown_task_...")  # Gets "qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac"
```

This `task_id` then propagates everywhere — into `GenerationTask.id`, into `reset_generation_started_at()`, into `update_task_status_supabase()`. All using the human-readable ID instead of the UUID.

**Even if we fix the edge function**, the client code at `worker.py:137` would still be fragile because it reads task_id from inside `params` rather than receiving the DB id directly. The proper fix is both:

1. **Server-side**: `claim-next-task` edge function should return the DB `id` column as `task_id`
2. **Client-side**: Always overwrite `params["task_id"]` with the UUID for ALL task types, not just orchestrators — move line 379 outside the `if` block

For comparison, the `check_my_assigned_tasks` recovery path (task_claim.py:126) correctly uses `task.get('id')` from the DB `id` column. Only the edge function claim path has this issue.

> AGENT

Here's the full picture:

**20 queued tasks, 0 being claimed.** The debug tool confirms:

**Task 78dfabdf** (qwen_image_style): Was claimed at 18:42:48 and actually completed at 18:57:53, but then got **reset back to Queued** with error "Reset - stuck in progress with no worker assigned". This happened because `worker_id` was null on the task row — the `claim-next-task` edge function's 500 error means it didn't properly set the worker_id.

**Task 9748d253** (z_image_turbo): Created at 18:01:50, never claimed. Just sitting in queue.

**Worker `imj8dnmlamj6tr`**: Heartbeating and active, but the orchestrator is marking it as error: "No Runpod ID" (because it's a local worker). Then: "Idle timeout (320s) with no work available" → terminated. The worker is alive and polling but can't claim anything because every `claim-next-task` call fails with the 500 error:

```
Could not choose the best candidate function between: 
  public.claim_next_task_user_pat(p_user_id => uuid, p_include_active => boolean), 
  public.claim_next_task_user_pat(p_user_id => uuid, p_include_active => boolean, p_run_type => text)
```

So the chain is:
1. Worker polls → `task-counts` says 20 queued → tries to claim
2. `claim-next-task` returns 500 (PostgreSQL overload ambiguity) → no task claimed
3. Worker sleeps, repeats forever
4. Orchestrator sees idle worker with no RunPod ID → marks as error → terminates

Everything leads back to the PostgreSQL function overload issue on the Supabase side. The old 2-parameter `claim_next_task_user_pat` needs to be dropped or the edge function needs to explicitly call the 3-parameter version.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - Continue debugging runtime issues with the deployed worker on the refactor/structural-cleanup branch
   - Fix three specific runtime bugs discovered during deployment testing:
     1. Silent claim failures (edge function errors hidden behind debug-only logging)
     2. LoRA multipliers not being applied correctly to LoRAs 2 and 3 (comma vs space separator)
     3. Task ID mismatch (using human-readable params.task_id instead of DB UUID for status updates)
   - Sense-check generation output logs for correctness
   - Move WGP-compatibility facades out of source/ into Wan2GP/ where they belong
   - Investigate why tasks aren't being claimed using the debug CLI tools

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **WGP LoRA multiplier parsing**: `preparse_loras_multipliers()` in `Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py` splits on spaces/newlines, NOT commas. Any comma-separated string becomes a single element.
   - **Task ID duality**: DB has `id` (UUID like `78dfabdf-...`) and `params.task_id` (human-readable like `qwen_image_style_26021217_003cac`). Edge functions expect the UUID.
   - **Edge function claim-next-task**: Returns correct UUID as `task_id` in response, but `worker.py:137` reads from `params["task_id"]` instead.
   - **PostgreSQL function overload**: `claim_next_task_user_pat` has 2-param and 3-param overloads that PostgREST can't disambiguate → 500 error. This is a server-side issue the user is investigating separately.
   - **dprint vs headless_logger**: `dprint()` only outputs when `debug_mode=True`, making critical errors invisible in production. Important failures should use `headless_logger.error()`.
   - **WGP sys.path imports**: Vendored WGP code adds directories to sys.path to import from source/. These should use direct qualified imports (`from source.media.structure.download import ...`) instead of facade modules.
   - **Debug CLI tool**: `python -m debug task <id>`, `python -m debug health`, `python -m debug worker <id>`, `python -m debug tasks --status Queued` — queries Supabase system_logs for task/worker investigation.

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`source/core/db/task_claim.py`** — Task claiming and assignment recovery
     - Changed `dprint()` calls to `headless_logger.error()` for critical failures (claim 500 errors, task counts failures)
     - Key change at line 323 (claim non-200 response):
       ```python
       # Was: dprint(f"Edge Function returned {resp.status_code}: {resp.text}")
       # Now:
       headless_logger.error(f"[CLAIM] Edge Function returned {resp.status_code}: {resp.text[:500]}")
       ```
     - Similar changes at lines 44, 54, 87, 92 for task_counts error paths

   - **`source/core/params/lora.py`** — LoRA configuration and WGP format conversion
     - Root cause of multiplier bug: `to_wgp_format()` line 285 used `','.join(multipliers)` producing `"0.9,1.1,0.5"`
     - Fixed to always use space-separated format:
       ```python
       # Was:
       has_phase_config = any(e.is_phase_config_multiplier() for e in ready_entries)
       if has_phase_config:
           loras_multipliers = ' '.join(multipliers)
       else:
           loras_multipliers = ','.join(multipliers)
       
       # Now:
       # WGP's preparse_loras_multipliers() splits on spaces, so both
       # phase-config ("0.9;0.5 1.1;1.1") and regular ("0.9 1.1 0.5")
       # must be space-separated
       loras_multipliers = ' '.join(multipliers)
       ```

   - **`source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py`** — WanOrchestrator generation pipeline
     - Added defensive comma→space replacement at line 718:
       ```python
       if isinstance(eff_multipliers, str):
           # WGP splits on spaces; replace commas defensively
           loras_multipliers_str = eff_multipliers.replace(',', ' ')
       ```

   - **`worker.py`** — Main worker polling loop
     - Fixed task_id overwrite to apply to ALL task types (was orchestrator-only):
       ```python
       # Was (line 377-381):
       if current_task_type in {"travel_orchestrator", "join_clips_orchestrator", ...}:
           current_task_params["task_id"] = current_task_id
           if "orchestrator_details" in current_task_params:
               current_task_params["orchestrator_details"]["orchestrator_task_id"] = current_task_id
       
       # Now:
       # Ensure params["task_id"] is the DB UUID (not the human-readable params.task_id)
       # process_single_task reads task_id from params, so this must be set for ALL task types
       current_task_params["task_id"] = current_task_id
       if "orchestrator_details" in current_task_params:
           current_task_params["orchestrator_details"]["orchestrator_task_id"] = current_task_id
       ```
     - The key problem: `process_single_task()` at line 137 reads `task_id = task_params_dict.get("task_id", ...)` from params, not from the top-level UUID returned by the edge function.

   - **`Wan2GP/models/wan/any2video.py`** — Vendored WGP video generation (lines 1659-1669)
     - Replaced sys.path hack that added `source/` directory with project root addition:
       ```python
       # Was:
       source_dir = Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.parent / "source"
       if str(source_dir) not in sys.path:
           sys.path.insert(0, str(source_dir))
       from structure_video_guidance import download_and_extract_motion_frames
       
       # Now:
       _project_root = str(Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.parent)
       if _project_root not in sys.path:
           sys.path.insert(0, _project_root)
       from source.media.structure.download import download_and_extract_motion_frames
       ```

   - **`source/structure_video_guidance.py`** — DELETED
     - Was a re-export facade only needed by any2video.py's old sys.path hack

     - Adds PROJECT_ROOT to sys.path, imports directly from `source.media.video.hires_utils`

   - **`source/db_operations.py`** — Re-export facade, was being read when summary was requested
     - Still exists as a facade re-exporting from `source.core.db.*` subpackages

   - **`source/task_handlers/queue/download_ops.py`** (lines 75-149) — Task conversion to WGP params
     - Calls `config.to_wgp_format()` which was producing comma-separated multipliers

   - **`source/task_handlers/tasks/task_conversion.py`** (line 235+) — DB task → GenerationTask conversion
     - Qwen handler sets `generation_params["lora_multipliers"]` as a list
     - `GenerationTask` created at line 526 with `parameters=generation_params`

   - **`Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py`** (lines 4-12) — WGP's multiplier parser
     - `preparse_loras_multipliers()` splits string input on newlines then spaces, NOT commas
     - This is vendored code we don't modify

   - **`source/core/db/config.py`** — DB configuration globals
     - `dprint()` function at line 47-50 only prints when `debug_mode=True`
     - `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` set by worker.py at startup

Here's the full timeline breakdown:

## qwen_image_style Task Timeline

| Phase | Run 1 (78dfabdf) | Run 2 (94167d53) | Run 3 (e38251f5) |
|-------|------------------|------------------|------------------|
| **Claim → params ready** | 19:26:57 → 19:32:47 (**5m 50s** — model load) | 19:40:28 → 19:40:30 (**2s** — already loaded) | 19:48:31 → 19:48:32 (**1s**) |
| **Params → "Extracting Raw Format"** | 19:32:47 → 19:39:27 (**6m 40s**) | 19:40:30 → 19:47:28 (**6m 58s**) | 19:48:32 → 19:55:31 (**6m 59s**) |
| **Denoising (pass 1, 8 steps)** | 19:39:27 → 19:39:57 (**30s**) | 19:47:28 → 19:47:54 (**26s**) | 19:55:31 → 19:55:58 (**27s**) |
| **Denoising (pass 2/hires, 8 steps)** | 19:39:57 → 19:40:18 (**21s**) | 19:47:54 → 19:48:21 (**27s**) | 19:55:58 → 19:56:12 (**14s**) |
| **Post-processing** | ~4s | ~4s | ~4s |
| **Total** | **13m 27s** | **7m 58s** | **7m 44s** |

The bottleneck is obvious: **~7 minutes of dead time** between sending params to WGP and the first denoising step. This happens consistently on every run (not just the first). The actual diffusion (2 passes x 8 steps) takes only ~45-55 seconds.

That 7-minute gap is inside WGP's `generate_video()` — after our code hands off params and before WGP reports "Extracting Raw Format". This is the **Qwen VLM (20B parameter) inference** step: processing the input/style images through the vision-language model to generate the conditioning embeddings. On a 4090 with 22GB VRAM, a 20B model likely requires heavy CPU offloading, which explains why it consistently takes ~7 minutes.

For comparison, z_image_turbo tasks (which don't have a VLM step) complete in ~23 seconds each after the model is loaded.

**Between-run overhead** is negligible — only 3-5 seconds between task completion and the next claim. The problem is entirely within the VLM inference inside WGP.

You're right to question this — it's NOT "extracting params". I was wrong about the cause. Let me show you what's actually happening.

The ~7 minute gap is **LoRA loading**. Here's the smoking gun:

**wgp.py:6439** — after every single generation:
```python
offload.unload_loras_from_model(trans_lora)
```

**wgp.py:5412** — at the start of every generation:
```python
offload.load_loras_into_model(trans_lora, loras_selected, ...)
```

WGP **unloads LoRAs after every generation**, then has to **reload them from scratch** for the next one. Loading 3 LoRAs into a 20B parameter Qwen model with CPU offloading (177GB/694GB RAM = the model is mostly on CPU) takes ~7 minutes each time.

The timeline for each run is:

| Phase | Time |
|-------|------|
| LoRA loading into 20B model (CPU-offloaded) | **~7 min** |
| Extracting Raw Format + prompt encoding | ~2s |
| Denoising (2 passes x 8 steps) | ~50s |
| **Total** | **~8 min** |

So to answer your question directly: it's NOT loaded from the previous time because WGP's `generate_video()` explicitly calls `offload.unload_loras_from_model()` at the end of every generation (line 6439). This is WGP's default behavior for UI use — where users might change LoRAs between runs. In headless batch mode running the same LoRAs 3x in a row, it's pure waste: 21 minutes of LoRA loading for ~2.5 minutes of actual generation.

Here's the audit summary:

## LoRA Handling: Current State

**Short answer: No, it's not standardized.** There's a well-designed `LoRAConfig`/`LoRAEntry` system in `source/core/params/lora.py`, but it's only consistently used in the queue-based task path. Several major code paths bypass it entirely:

### What's Clean
- **Queue task path**: `TaskConfig.from_params()` → `LoRAConfig.from_params()` → download → `mark_downloaded()` → `to_wgp_format()` — fully typed, status-tracked

### What's Messy

1. **Qwen handler** — completely bypasses LoRAConfig. Manually appends filenames/multipliers to raw lists, does its own HF downloads via `_download_lora_if_missing()`. None of this is tracked through LoRAEntry status.

2. **Phase multiplier construction** — duplicated in 3 places: `parse_phase_config()` in task_conversion.py (manual string formatting), `qwen_handler.py` (uses the helper), and `lora_validation.py` (raw string splitting). Should all use `format_phase_multipliers()`.

3. **Travel orchestrator SVI merge** — raw array concatenation (`lora_names[]` + `lora_multipliers[]`) without touching LoRAConfig. The task_registry creates a LoRAConfig for validation then immediately extracts it back to raw arrays.

4. **Format ambiguity** — multipliers appear as comma-separated, space-separated, and semicolon-separated in different places. The to_wgp_format() fix from earlier (comma → space) was a symptom of this.

### Root Problem

The codebase is in transition — `LoRAConfig` was designed as the canonical representation, but predates parts of the codebase that were written with raw dict/string manipulation. The fix would be to route all LoRA entry points through `LoRAConfig` and stop extracting back to raw arrays. The Qwen handler is the biggest offender.

Want me to plan a cleanup to standardize this?
